<p>The <b>Faroe Islands</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;f&#39; in &#39;find&#39;">f</span><span title="/ɛər/: &#39;are&#39; in &#39;bare&#39;">ɛər</span><span title="/oʊ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;code&#39;">oʊ</span></span>/</a></span></span> <a href="/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key" title="Help:Pronunciation respelling key"><i title="English pronunciation respelling"><span style="font-size:90%">FAIR</span>-oh</i></a>), or simply the <b>Faroes</b> (<a href="/wiki/Faroese_language" title="Faroese language">Faroese</a>: <i lang="fo">Føroyar</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="fo-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA" title="Help:IPA">[ˈfœɹjaɹ]</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:Fo-Foroyar.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/0c/Fo-Foroyar.ogg" class="internal" title="Fo-Foroyar.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span>; <a href="/wiki/Danish_language" title="Danish language">Danish</a>: <i lang="da">Færøerne</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="da-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Danish" title="Help:IPA/Danish">[ˈfeɐ̯ˌøˀɐnə]</a></span>), are a <a href="/wiki/North_Atlantic" class="mw-redirect" title="North Atlantic">North Atlantic</a> <a href="/wiki/Archipelago" title="Archipelago">island group</a> and an autonomous territory of the <a href="/wiki/Danish_Realm" title="Danish Realm">Kingdom of Denmark</a>.
</p><p>They are located 320 kilometres (200&#160;mi) north-northwest of the <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">United Kingdom</a>, and about halfway between <a href="/wiki/Norway" title="Norway">Norway</a> (580 kilometres (360&#160;mi) away) and <a href="/wiki/Iceland" title="Iceland">Iceland</a> (430 kilometres (270&#160;mi) away). The islands form part of the Kingdom of Denmark, along with mainland <a href="/wiki/Denmark" title="Denmark">Denmark</a> and <a href="/wiki/Greenland" title="Greenland">Greenland</a>. The islands have a total area of about 1,400 square kilometres (540&#160;sq&#160;mi) with a population of 54,000 as of June 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The terrain is rugged, and the <a href="/wiki/Oceanic_climate#Subpolar_variety_(Cfc)" title="Oceanic climate">subpolar oceanic climate (Cfc)</a> is windy, wet, cloudy, and cool. Temperatures for such a northerly climate are moderated by the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_Stream" title="Gulf Stream">Gulf Stream</a>, averaging above freezing throughout the year, and hovering around 12&#160;°C (54&#160;°F) in summer and 5&#160;°C (41&#160;°F) in winter.<sup id="cite_ref-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> The northerly latitude also results in perpetual <a href="/wiki/Civil_twilight" class="mw-redirect" title="Civil twilight">civil twilight</a> during summer nights and very short winter days.
</p><p>Between 1035 and 1814, the Faroe Islands were part of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Norway_(872%E2%80%931397)" title="Kingdom of Norway (872–1397)">Kingdom of Norway</a>, which was in a <a href="/wiki/Personal_union" title="Personal union">personal union</a> with Denmark from 1380. In 1814, the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Kiel" title="Treaty of Kiel">Treaty of Kiel</a> transferred Norway to Sweden, whereas Denmark kept its Atlantic territories, which included the Faroe Islands, <a href="/wiki/Greenland" title="Greenland">Greenland</a> and <a href="/wiki/Iceland" title="Iceland">Iceland</a>.
</p><p>While part of the Kingdom of Denmark, the Faroe Islands have been <a href="/wiki/Self-governance" title="Self-governance">self-governing</a> since 1948,<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> controlling most areas apart from <a href="/wiki/Danish_Defence" title="Danish Defence">military defence</a>, <a href="/wiki/Police_of_Denmark" title="Police of Denmark">policing</a>, <a href="/wiki/Justice_Minister_of_Denmark" class="mw-redirect" title="Justice Minister of Denmark">justice</a>, <a href="/wiki/Currency" title="Currency">currency</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Foreign_Affairs_(Denmark)" title="Minister of Foreign Affairs (Denmark)">foreign affairs</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Because the Faroe Islands are not part of the same <a href="/wiki/Customs_area" class="mw-redirect" title="Customs area">customs area</a> as Denmark, the Faroe Islands have an independent <a href="/wiki/Commercial_policy" title="Commercial policy">trade policy</a>, and can establish trade agreements with other states. The Faroes have an extensive bilateral free trade agreement with Iceland, known as the <a href="/wiki/Hoyv%C3%ADk_Agreement" title="Hoyvík Agreement">Hoyvík Agreement</a>. In the <a href="/wiki/Nordic_Council" title="Nordic Council">Nordic Council</a>, they are represented as part of the Danish delegation. In certain sports, the Faroe Islands field their own national teams. They did not become a part of the <a href="/wiki/European_Economic_Community" title="European Economic Community">European Economic Community</a> in 1973, instead keeping the <a href="/wiki/Autonomy" title="Autonomy">autonomy</a> over their own fishing waters.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Etymology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>In <a href="/wiki/Faroese_language" title="Faroese language">Faroese</a>, the name appears as <span title="Faroese-language text"><i lang="fo">Føroyar</i></span>. <span title="Faroese-language text"><i lang="fo">Oyar</i></span> represents the plural of <span title="Faroese-language text"><i lang="fo">oy</i></span>, older Faroese for 'island'. Due to sound changes, the modern Faroese word for island is <span title="Faroese-language text"><i lang="fo">oyggj</i></span>. The first element, <span title="Faroese-language text"><i lang="fo">før</i></span>, may reflect an Old Norse word <span title="Old Norse-language text"><i lang="non">fær</i></span> ('sheep'), although this analysis is sometimes disputed because Faroese now uses the word <span title="Faroese-language text"><i lang="fo">seyður</i></span> (from Old Norse <span title="Old Norse-language text"><i lang="non">sauðr</i></span>) to mean 'sheep'. Another possibility is that the Irish monks, who settled the island around 625, had already given the islands a name related to the Gaelic word <span title="Irish-language text"><i lang="ga">fearann</i></span>, meaning 'land' or 'estate'. This name could then have been passed on to the Norwegian settlers, who then added <span title="Faroese-language text"><i lang="fo">oyar</i></span> (islands).<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> The name thus translates as either 'Islands of Sheep' or 'Land Islands'.
</p><p>In English, it may be seen as a <a href="/wiki/Tautology_(language)" title="Tautology (language)">tautology</a> to say <i>the Faroe Islands</i>, since the <i>oe</i> comes from an element meaning 'island'. This is seen in the <a href="/wiki/BBC" title="BBC">BBC</a> <a href="/wiki/Shipping_Forecast" title="Shipping Forecast">Shipping Forecast</a>, where the waters around the islands are called <i>Faeroes</i>. The name is also rarely spelled <i>Faeroe</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="History of the Faroe Islands">History of the Faroe Islands</a></div>
<p>There is some evidence of settlement on the Faroe Islands before Norse <a href="/wiki/Viking" class="mw-redirect" title="Viking">Viking</a> settlers arrived in the <a href="/wiki/Ninth_century" class="mw-redirect" title="Ninth century">ninth century</a> <a href="/wiki/AD" class="mw-redirect" title="AD">AD</a>. Archeologists found burnt grains of domesticated <a href="/wiki/Barley" title="Barley">barley</a> and <a href="/wiki/Peat" title="Peat">peat</a> ash deposited in two phases; the first dated between the mid-fourth and mid-sixth centuries, and another between the late-sixth and late-eighth centuries.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> Researchers have also found sheep DNA in lake-bed sediments, which were dated to around the year 500. Barley and sheep had to have been brought to the islands by humans. As Scandinavians did not begin using the <a href="/wiki/Sail" title="Sail">sail</a> until about 750, it is unlikely they could have reached the Faroes before then, and it is more likely the settlers came from Great Britain or Ireland.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Archaeologist Mike Church noted that <a href="/wiki/Dicuil" title="Dicuil">Dicuil</a>, an Irish monk of the early ninth century, may have mentioned the Faroes. Dicuil wrote in his geographical work <i>De mensura orbis terrae</i> that there were <i>heremitae ex nostra Scotia</i> ("<a href="/wiki/Hermit" title="Hermit">hermits</a> from our land of Ireland/Scotland") who had lived on the northerly islands of Britain for almost a hundred years until the Vikings arrived.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Church suggested that the people living there might have been from Ireland, Scotland, or Scandinavia, or possibly from all three.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> According to a ninth-century <a href="/wiki/Immram" title="Immram">voyage tale</a>, the Irish saint <a href="/wiki/Brendan_the_Navigator" title="Brendan the Navigator">Brendan</a> visited islands resembling the Faroes in the sixth century. This association, however, is not conclusive.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Norsemen settled the islands c. 800, bringing <a href="/wiki/Old_West_Norse" class="mw-redirect" title="Old West Norse">Old West Norse</a>, which evolved into the modern <a href="/wiki/Faroese_language" title="Faroese language">Faroese language</a>. According to Icelandic sagas such as <i>Færeyjar Saga</i>, one of the best known men in the island was <a href="/wiki/Tr%C3%B3ndur_%C3%AD_G%C3%B8tu" title="Tróndur í Gøtu">Tróndur í Gøtu</a>, a descendant of Scandinavian chiefs who had settled in <a href="/wiki/Dublin" title="Dublin">Dublin</a>, Ireland. Tróndur led the battle against <a href="/wiki/Sigmund_Brestisson" class="mw-redirect" title="Sigmund Brestisson">Sigmund Brestisson</a>, the Norwegian monarchy and the Norwegian church.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Faroe_Islands,_1767,_as_seen_by_Yves_de_Kerguelen_Tr%C3%A9marec.PNG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Faroe_Islands%2C_1767%2C_as_seen_by_Yves_de_Kerguelen_Tr%C3%A9marec.PNG/220px-Faroe_Islands%2C_1767%2C_as_seen_by_Yves_de_Kerguelen_Tr%C3%A9marec.PNG" decoding="async" width="220" height="122" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Faroe_Islands%2C_1767%2C_as_seen_by_Yves_de_Kerguelen_Tr%C3%A9marec.PNG/330px-Faroe_Islands%2C_1767%2C_as_seen_by_Yves_de_Kerguelen_Tr%C3%A9marec.PNG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8e/Faroe_Islands%2C_1767%2C_as_seen_by_Yves_de_Kerguelen_Tr%C3%A9marec.PNG/440px-Faroe_Islands%2C_1767%2C_as_seen_by_Yves_de_Kerguelen_Tr%C3%A9marec.PNG 2x" data-file-width="1254" data-file-height="693" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Faroe_Islands,_1767,_as_seen_by_Yves_de_Kerguelen_Tr%C3%A9marec.PNG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The Faroe Islands as seen by the Breton navigator <a href="/wiki/Yves-Joseph_de_Kerguelen-Tr%C3%A9marec" title="Yves-Joseph de Kerguelen-Trémarec">Yves-Joseph de Kerguelen-Trémarec</a> in 1767</div></div></div>
<p>The Norse and <a href="/wiki/Norse%E2%80%93Gaels" title="Norse–Gaels">Norse–Gael</a> settlers probably did not come directly from Scandinavia, but rather from Norse communities surrounding the <a href="/wiki/Irish_Sea" title="Irish Sea">Irish Sea</a>, <a href="/wiki/Northern_Isles" title="Northern Isles">Northern Isles</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Outer_Hebrides" title="Outer Hebrides">Outer Hebrides</a> of <a href="/wiki/Scotland" title="Scotland">Scotland</a>, including the <a href="/wiki/Shetland" title="Shetland">Shetland</a> and <a href="/wiki/Orkney" title="Orkney">Orkney</a> islands. A traditional name for the islands in <a href="/wiki/Irish_language" title="Irish language">Irish</a>, <i>Na Scigirí</i>, possibly refers to the <i>(Eyja-)Skeggjar</i> "(Island-)Beards", a nickname given to island dwellers.
</p><p>According to the <i><a href="/wiki/F%C3%A6reyinga_saga" title="Færeyinga saga">Færeyinga saga</a></i>, more emigrants left Norway who did not approve of the monarchy of <a href="/wiki/Harald_Fairhair" title="Harald Fairhair">Harald Fairhair</a> (ruled c. 872 to 930). These people settled the Faroes around the end of the ninth century.<sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> Early in the eleventh century, <a href="/wiki/Sigmundur_Brestisson" title="Sigmundur Brestisson">Sigmundur Brestisson</a> (961–1005) – whose clan had flourished in the southern islands before invaders from the northern islands almost <a href="/wiki/Genocide" title="Genocide">exterminated</a> it – escaped to Norway. He was sent back to take possession of the islands for <a href="/wiki/Olaf_Tryggvason" title="Olaf Tryggvason">Olaf Tryggvason</a>, <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_Norway" title="Monarchy of Norway">King of Norway</a> from 995 to 1000.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Sigmundur introduced Christianity, forcing Tróndur í Gøtu to convert or face beheading and, although Sigmundur was subsequently murdered, Norwegian taxation was upheld. Norwegian control of the Faroes continued until 1814, although, when the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Norway_(872%E2%80%931397)" title="Kingdom of Norway (872–1397)">Kingdom of Norway (872–1397)</a> entered the <a href="/wiki/Kalmar_Union" title="Kalmar Union">Kalmar Union</a> with Denmark, this gradually resulted in Danish control of the islands. The <a href="/wiki/Protestant_Reformation" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestant Reformation">Protestant Reformation</a> in the form of <a href="/wiki/Lutheranism" title="Lutheranism">Lutheranism</a> reached the Faroes in 1538. When the union between Denmark and Norway was dissolved as a result of the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Kiel" title="Treaty of Kiel">Treaty of Kiel</a> in 1814, Denmark retained possession of the Faroe Islands (along with Greenland and Iceland); Norway itself was joined in a union with Sweden.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Kirkjub%C3%B8ur,_Faroe_Islands.JPG" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Kirkjub%C3%B8ur%2C_Faroe_Islands.JPG/300px-Kirkjub%C3%B8ur%2C_Faroe_Islands.JPG" decoding="async" width="300" height="200" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Kirkjub%C3%B8ur%2C_Faroe_Islands.JPG/450px-Kirkjub%C3%B8ur%2C_Faroe_Islands.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a6/Kirkjub%C3%B8ur%2C_Faroe_Islands.JPG/600px-Kirkjub%C3%B8ur%2C_Faroe_Islands.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2560" data-file-height="1704" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Kirkjub%C3%B8ur,_Faroe_Islands.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Ruins of the Cathedral of <a href="/wiki/Magnus_of_Orkney" class="mw-redirect" title="Magnus of Orkney">St. Magnus of Orkney</a></div></div></div>
<p>Following the turmoil caused by the <a href="/wiki/Napoleonic_Wars" title="Napoleonic Wars">Napoleonic Wars</a> (1803–1815), in 1816, the Faroe Islands became a county within the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Denmark" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Denmark">Danish Kingdom</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As part of its <a href="/wiki/Mercantilism" title="Mercantilism">mercantilist</a> economic policy, Denmark maintained a <a href="/wiki/Danish_Royal_Trade_Monopoly_in_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Danish Royal Trade Monopoly in the Faroe Islands">monopoly over trade</a> with the Faroe Islands and forbade their inhabitants trading with others (e.g. the geographically close Britain). The trade monopoly in the Faroe Islands was abolished in 1856, after which the area developed as a modern fishing nation with its own <a href="/wiki/Fishing_fleet" title="Fishing fleet">fishing fleet</a>. The national awakening from 1888 initially arose from a struggle to maintain the <a href="/wiki/Faroese_language" title="Faroese language">Faroese language</a> and was thus <a href="/wiki/Culture" title="Culture">culturally</a> oriented, but after 1906 it became more <a href="/wiki/Politics" title="Politics">political</a> with the foundation of <a href="/wiki/List_of_political_parties_in_the_Faroe_Islands" title="List of political parties in the Faroe Islands">political parties within the Faroe Islands</a>.
</p><p>In the first year of the <a href="/wiki/Second_World_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Second World War">Second World War</a>, on 12 April 1940, <a href="/wiki/British_occupation_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="British occupation of the Faroe Islands">British troops occupied the Faroe Islands in Operation Valentine</a>. <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">Nazi Germany</a> had invaded Denmark and commenced the invasion of Norway on 9 April 1940 under <a href="/wiki/Operation_Weser%C3%BCbung" title="Operation Weserübung">Operation Weserübung</a>. In 1942–1943, the British <a href="/wiki/Royal_Monmouthshire_Royal_Engineers" title="Royal Monmouthshire Royal Engineers">Royal Engineers</a>, under the command of <a href="/wiki/Lieutenant_colonel" title="Lieutenant colonel">lieutenant colonel</a> William Law, built the first and only airport in the Faroe Islands, <a href="/wiki/V%C3%A1gar_Airport" title="Vágar Airport">Vágar Airport</a>. Following the war, control of the islands reverted to Denmark, but Danish rule had been undermined, and <a href="/wiki/Iceland" title="Iceland">Iceland</a>'s full independence served as a precedent and a model in the mind of many Faroese.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/1946_Faroese_independence_referendum" title="1946 Faroese independence referendum">1946 Faroese independence referendum</a> resulted in 50.73% in favour of independence to 49.27% against.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> The Faroe Islands subsequently declared independence on 18 September 1946; however, this declaration was annulled by <a href="/wiki/Denmark" title="Denmark">Denmark</a> on 20 September on the grounds that a majority of the Faroese voters had not supported independence and <a href="/wiki/Christian_X_of_Denmark" title="Christian X of Denmark">King Christian X of Denmark</a> dissolved the Faroese <a href="/wiki/L%C3%B8gting" title="Løgting">Løgting</a> on 24 September.<sup id="cite_ref-Steining185_27-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Steining185-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> The dissolution of the Løgting took place in November, followed by the <a href="/wiki/1946_Faroese_parliamentary_election" class="mw-redirect" title="1946 Faroese parliamentary election">Faroese parliamentary election of 1946</a> in which the parties in favour of full independence received a total of 5,396 votes while the parties opposed to it received a total of 7,488 votes.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> As a reaction to the growing movements in favour of self-government and independence, Denmark finally granted the Faroe Islands <a href="/wiki/Home-rule" class="mw-redirect" title="Home-rule">home-rule</a> with a high degree of local autonomy in 1948.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_29-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1973 the Faroe Islands declined to join Denmark in entering the <a href="/wiki/European_Economic_Community" title="European Economic Community">European Economic Community</a> (which later became the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>). Following the collapse of the fishing industry in the early 1990s, the Faroes experienced considerable economic difficulties.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Geography of the Faroe Islands">Geography of the Faroe Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Faroe_Islands_by_Sentinel-2.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Faroe_Islands_by_Sentinel-2.jpg/220px-Faroe_Islands_by_Sentinel-2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="301" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Faroe_Islands_by_Sentinel-2.jpg/330px-Faroe_Islands_by_Sentinel-2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/Faroe_Islands_by_Sentinel-2.jpg/440px-Faroe_Islands_by_Sentinel-2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4729" data-file-height="6460" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Faroe_Islands_by_Sentinel-2.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Satellite image of the Faroe Islands</div></div></div>
<p>The Faroe Islands are an island group consisting of <a href="/wiki/List_of_islands_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="List of islands of the Faroe Islands">18 major islands</a> (and a total of 779 islands, <a href="/wiki/Islet" title="Islet">islets</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Skerry" title="Skerry">skerries</a>) about 655 kilometres (407&#160;mi) off the coast of Northern Europe, between the <a href="/wiki/Norwegian_Sea" title="Norwegian Sea">Norwegian Sea</a> and the <a href="/wiki/North_Atlantic_Ocean" class="mw-redirect" title="North Atlantic Ocean">North Atlantic Ocean</a>, about halfway between <a href="/wiki/Iceland" title="Iceland">Iceland</a> and <a href="/wiki/Norway" title="Norway">Norway</a>, the closest neighbours being the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Isles" title="Northern Isles">Northern Isles</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Outer_Hebrides" title="Outer Hebrides">Outer Hebrides</a> of <a href="/wiki/Scotland" title="Scotland">Scotland</a>. Its coordinates are <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1073938472"/><span class="plainlinks nourlexpansion"><a class="external text" href="//geohack.toolforge.org/geohack.php?pagename=Faroe_Islands&amp;params=62_00_N_06_47_W_"><span class="geo-default"><span class="geo-dms" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location"><span class="latitude">62°00′N</span> <span class="longitude">06°47′W</span></span></span><span class="geo-multi-punct">&#xfeff; / &#xfeff;</span><span class="geo-nondefault"><span class="geo-dec" title="Maps, aerial photos, and other data for this location">62.000°N 6.783°W</span><span style="display:none">&#xfeff; / <span class="geo">62.000; -6.783</span></span></span></a></span>.
</p><p>Distance from the Faroe Islands to:
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/North_Rona" title="North Rona">Rona</a>, Scotland (uninhabited): 260 kilometres (160&#160;mi)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Shetland" title="Shetland">Shetland</a> (<a href="/wiki/Foula" title="Foula">Foula</a>), Scotland: 285 kilometres (177&#160;mi)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Orkney" title="Orkney">Orkney</a> (<a href="/wiki/Westray" title="Westray">Westray</a>), Scotland: 300 kilometres (190&#160;mi)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Scotland" title="Scotland">Scotland</a> (mainland): 320 kilometres (200&#160;mi)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Iceland" title="Iceland">Iceland</a>: 450 kilometres (280&#160;mi)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Norway" title="Norway">Norway</a>: 590 kilometres (370&#160;mi)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Ireland" title="Ireland">Ireland</a>: 670 kilometres (420&#160;mi)</li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Denmark" title="Denmark">Denmark</a>: 990 kilometres (620&#160;mi)</li></ul>
<p>The islands cover an area of 1,399 square kilometres (540&#160;sq.&#160;mi) and have <a href="/wiki/List_of_lakes_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="List of lakes of the Faroe Islands">small lakes</a> and rivers, but no major ones. There are 1,117 kilometres (694&#160;mi) of coastline.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_Factbook_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_Factbook-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> The only significant uninhabited island is <a href="/wiki/L%C3%ADtla_D%C3%ADmun" title="Lítla Dímun">Lítla Dímun</a>.
</p><p>The islands are rugged and rocky with some low peaks; the coasts are mostly cliffs. The highest point is <a href="/wiki/Sl%C3%A6ttaratindur" title="Slættaratindur">Slættaratindur</a> in northern <a href="/wiki/Eysturoy" title="Eysturoy">Eysturoy</a>, 882 metres (2,894&#160;ft) <a href="/wiki/Above_sea_level" class="mw-redirect" title="Above sea level">above sea level</a>.
</p><p>The Faroe Islands are made up of an approximately six-kilometres-thick succession of mostly <a href="/wiki/Basalt" title="Basalt">basaltic</a> <a href="/wiki/Lava" title="Lava">lava</a> that was part of the great <a href="/wiki/North_Atlantic_Igneous_Province" title="North Atlantic Igneous Province">North Atlantic Igneous Province</a> during the <a href="/wiki/Paleogene" title="Paleogene">Paleogene</a> period.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> The lavas were erupted during the opening of the <a href="/wiki/North_Atlantic_ocean" class="mw-redirect" title="North Atlantic ocean">North Atlantic ocean</a>, which began about 60 million years ago, and what is today the Faroe Islands was then attached to Greenland.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> The lavas are underlain by circa 30&#160;km of unidentified ancient continental crust.<sup id="cite_ref-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_the_Faroe_Islands#Climate" title="Geography of the Faroe Islands">Geography of the Faroe Islands §&#160;Climate</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Faroe_Islands,_Eysturoy,_Skipanes_and_Undir_G%C3%B8tuei%C3%B0i.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Faroe_Islands%2C_Eysturoy%2C_Skipanes_and_Undir_G%C3%B8tuei%C3%B0i.jpg/290px-Faroe_Islands%2C_Eysturoy%2C_Skipanes_and_Undir_G%C3%B8tuei%C3%B0i.jpg" decoding="async" width="290" height="193" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Faroe_Islands%2C_Eysturoy%2C_Skipanes_and_Undir_G%C3%B8tuei%C3%B0i.jpg/435px-Faroe_Islands%2C_Eysturoy%2C_Skipanes_and_Undir_G%C3%B8tuei%C3%B0i.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cf/Faroe_Islands%2C_Eysturoy%2C_Skipanes_and_Undir_G%C3%B8tuei%C3%B0i.jpg/580px-Faroe_Islands%2C_Eysturoy%2C_Skipanes_and_Undir_G%C3%B8tuei%C3%B0i.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1930" data-file-height="1282" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Faroe_Islands,_Eysturoy,_Skipanes_and_Undir_G%C3%B8tuei%C3%B0i.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The village of <a href="/wiki/Skipanes" title="Skipanes">Skipanes</a> on <a href="/wiki/Eysturoy" title="Eysturoy">Eysturoy</a>, with different weather in the distance</div></div></div>
<p>The climate is classed as <a href="/wiki/Subpolar_oceanic_climate" class="mw-redirect" title="Subpolar oceanic climate">subpolar oceanic climate</a> according to the <a href="/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification" title="Köppen climate classification">Köppen climate classification</a>: <a href="/wiki/Oceanic_climate" title="Oceanic climate">Cfc</a>, with areas having a <a href="/wiki/Tundra" title="Tundra">tundra</a> climate, especially in the mountains, although some coastal or low-lying areas may have very mild-winter versions of a tundra climate. The overall character of the climate of the islands is influenced by the strong warming influence of the Atlantic Ocean, which produces the <a href="/wiki/North_Atlantic_Current" title="North Atlantic Current">North Atlantic Current</a>. This, together with the remoteness of any source of landmass-induced warm or cold airflows, ensures that winters are mild (mean temperature 3.0 to 4.0&#160;°C or 37 to 39&#160;°F) while summers are cool (mean temperature 9.5 to 10.5&#160;°C or 49 to 51&#160;°F).
</p><p>The islands are windy, cloudy, and cool throughout the year with an average of 210 rainy or snowy days per year. The islands lie in the path of depressions moving northeast, making strong winds and heavy rain possible at all times of the year. Sunny days are rare and overcast days are common. <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Faith" title="Hurricane Faith">Hurricane Faith</a> struck the Faroe Islands on 5 September 1966 with sustained winds over 100&#160;mph (160&#160;km/h) and only then did the storm cease to be a tropical system.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:352px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Faroe_Islands,_Eysturoy,_Funningsfj%C3%B8r%C3%B0ur_(fjord)_in_October.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Faroe_Islands%2C_Eysturoy%2C_Funningsfj%C3%B8r%C3%B0ur_%28fjord%29_in_October.jpg/350px-Faroe_Islands%2C_Eysturoy%2C_Funningsfj%C3%B8r%C3%B0ur_%28fjord%29_in_October.jpg" decoding="async" width="350" height="151" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Faroe_Islands%2C_Eysturoy%2C_Funningsfj%C3%B8r%C3%B0ur_%28fjord%29_in_October.jpg/525px-Faroe_Islands%2C_Eysturoy%2C_Funningsfj%C3%B8r%C3%B0ur_%28fjord%29_in_October.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Faroe_Islands%2C_Eysturoy%2C_Funningsfj%C3%B8r%C3%B0ur_%28fjord%29_in_October.jpg/700px-Faroe_Islands%2C_Eysturoy%2C_Funningsfj%C3%B8r%C3%B0ur_%28fjord%29_in_October.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2144" data-file-height="928" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Faroe_Islands,_Eysturoy,_Funningsfj%C3%B8r%C3%B0ur_(fjord)_in_October.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>An October evening on <a href="/wiki/Eysturoy" title="Eysturoy">Eysturoy</a></div></div></div>
<p>The climate varies greatly over small distances, due to the altitude, ocean currents, topography, and winds. Precipitation varies considerably throughout the archipelago. In some highland areas, snow cover may last for months with snowfalls possible for the greater part of the year (on the highest peaks, summer snowfall is by no means rare), while in some sheltered coastal locations, several years pass without any snowfall whatsoever. Tórshavn receives frosts more often than other areas just a short distance to the south. Snow also is seen at a much higher frequency than on outlying islands nearby. The area receives on average 49 frosts a year.<sup id="cite_ref-DMI_38-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DMI-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The collection of meteorological data on the Faroe Islands began in 1867.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> Winter recording began in 1891, and the warmest winter occurred in 2016–17 with an average temperature of 6.1&#160;°C (43&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Flora">Flora</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Flora">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Flora_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Flora of the Faroe Islands">Flora of the Faroe Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:262px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Myrisolja.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/35/Myrisolja.jpg/260px-Myrisolja.jpg" decoding="async" width="260" height="195" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/35/Myrisolja.jpg/390px-Myrisolja.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/3/35/Myrisolja.jpg/520px-Myrisolja.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="768" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Myrisolja.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Marsh marigold (<i><a href="/wiki/Caltha_palustris" title="Caltha palustris">Caltha palustris</a></i>) is common in the Faroe Islands during May and June.</div></div></div>
<p>The Faroes belong to the <a href="/wiki/Faroe_Islands_boreal_grasslands" title="Faroe Islands boreal grasslands">Faroe Islands boreal grasslands</a> ecoregion.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> The natural vegetation of the Faroe Islands is dominated by arctic-alpine plants, wildflowers, grasses, moss, and lichen. Most of the lowland area is grassland and some is heath, dominated by shrubby heathers, mainly <a href="/wiki/Calluna" title="Calluna"><i>Calluna vulgaris</i></a>. Among the herbaceous flora that occur in the Faroe Islands is the cosmopolitan marsh thistle, <i><a href="/wiki/Cirsium_palustre" title="Cirsium palustre">Cirsium palustre</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Although there are no trees native to the Faroe Islands, a limited number of species have been successfully introduced to the region, in particular trees from the <a href="/wiki/Magellanic_subpolar_forests" title="Magellanic subpolar forests">Magellanic subpolar forests</a> region of Chile. Conditions in the Magellanic subpolar forests are similar to those in the Faroe Islands, with cold summers and near-continuous subpolar winds. The following species from <a href="/wiki/Tierra_del_Fuego" title="Tierra del Fuego">Tierra del Fuego</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/Drimys_winteri" title="Drimys winteri">Drimys winteri</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Nothofagus_antarctica" title="Nothofagus antarctica">Nothofagus antarctica</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Nothofagus_pumilio" title="Nothofagus pumilio">Nothofagus pumilio</a></i>, and <i><a href="/wiki/Nothofagus_betuloides" title="Nothofagus betuloides">Nothofagus betuloides</a></i>, have been successfully introduced to the Faroe Islands. A non-Chilean species that has been introduced is the <a href="/wiki/Populus_trichocarpa#Distribution" title="Populus trichocarpa">black cottonwood</a>, also known as the California poplar (<i>Populus trichocarpa</i>).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (January 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>A collection of Faroese marine <a href="/wiki/Algae" title="Algae">algae</a> resulting from a survey sponsored by <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>,<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2017)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> the <a href="/wiki/British_Museum" title="British Museum">British Museum</a> (Natural History) and the Carlsberg Foundation, is preserved in the <a href="/wiki/Ulster_Museum" title="Ulster Museum">Ulster Museum</a> (catalogue numbers: F3195–F3307). It is one of ten <a href="/wiki/Exsiccatae" class="mw-redirect" title="Exsiccatae">exsiccatae</a> sets. A few small plantations consisting of plants collected from similar climates such as <a href="/wiki/Tierra_del_Fuego" title="Tierra del Fuego">Tierra del Fuego</a> in South America and <a href="/wiki/Alaska" title="Alaska">Alaska</a> thrive on the islands.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Fauna">Fauna</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Fauna">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Fauna_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Fauna of the Faroe Islands">Fauna of the Faroe Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:262px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Dreierprofil.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Dreierprofil.jpg/260px-Dreierprofil.jpg" decoding="async" width="260" height="173" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Dreierprofil.jpg/390px-Dreierprofil.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Dreierprofil.jpg/520px-Dreierprofil.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3504" data-file-height="2336" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Dreierprofil.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Atlantic_puffin" title="Atlantic puffin">Atlantic puffins</a> are very common and a part of the local cuisine: <a href="/wiki/Faroese_puffin" class="mw-redirect" title="Faroese puffin">Faroese puffin</a>.</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_birds_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="List of birds of the Faroe Islands">List of birds of the Faroe Islands</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/List_of_birds_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="List of birds of the Faroe Islands">bird fauna of the Faroe Islands</a> is dominated by <a href="/wiki/Seabird" title="Seabird">seabirds</a> and birds attracted to open land such as <a href="/wiki/Calluna" title="Calluna">heather</a>, probably because of the lack of woodland and other suitable habitats. Many species have developed special Faroese sub-species: <a href="/wiki/Common_eider" title="Common eider">common eider</a>, <a href="/wiki/Common_starling" title="Common starling">Common starling</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eurasian_wren" title="Eurasian wren">Eurasian wren</a>, <a href="/wiki/Common_murre" title="Common murre">common murre</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Black_guillemot" title="Black guillemot">black guillemot</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Pied_raven" title="Pied raven">pied raven</a>, a colour morph of the North Atlantic subspecies of the <a href="/wiki/Common_raven" title="Common raven">common raven</a>, was <a href="/wiki/Endemism" title="Endemism">endemic</a> to the Faroe Islands, but now has become extinct.
</p><p>Only a few species of wild land mammals are found in the Faroe Islands today, all introduced by humans. Three species are thriving on the islands today: <a href="/wiki/Mountain_hare" title="Mountain hare">mountain hare</a> (<i>Lepus timidus</i>), <a href="/wiki/Brown_rat" title="Brown rat">brown rat</a> (<i>Rattus norvegicus</i>), and the <a href="/wiki/House_mouse" title="House mouse">house mouse</a> (<i>Mus musculus</i>). Apart from these, there is a local domestic sheep breed, the <a href="/wiki/Faroe_sheep" title="Faroe sheep">Faroe sheep</a> (depicted on the <a href="/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Coat of arms of the Faroe Islands">coat of arms</a>), and there once was a variety of <a href="/wiki/Feral_animal" class="mw-redirect" title="Feral animal">feral sheep</a>, which survived on <a href="/wiki/L%C3%ADtla_D%C3%ADmun" title="Lítla Dímun">Lítla Dímun</a> until the mid-nineteenth century.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Faroese_sheep_Sumba_1.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Faroese_sheep_Sumba_1.jpg/290px-Faroese_sheep_Sumba_1.jpg" decoding="async" width="290" height="201" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Faroese_sheep_Sumba_1.jpg/435px-Faroese_sheep_Sumba_1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0e/Faroese_sheep_Sumba_1.jpg/580px-Faroese_sheep_Sumba_1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5030" data-file-height="3490" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Faroese_sheep_Sumba_1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Faroe_sheep" title="Faroe sheep">Faroe sheep</a> with the town of <a href="/wiki/Sumba,_Faroe_Islands" title="Sumba, Faroe Islands">Sumba</a> in the background.</div></div></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Grey_seal" title="Grey seal">Grey seals</a> (<i>Halichoerus grypus</i>) are common around the shorelines.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (July 2011)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Several species of <a href="/wiki/Cetacea" title="Cetacea">cetacea</a> live in the waters around the Faroe Islands. Best known are the <a href="/wiki/Long-finned_pilot_whale" title="Long-finned pilot whale">long-finned pilot whales</a> (<i>Globicephala melaena</i>), which still are hunted by the islanders in accordance with longstanding local tradition.<sup id="cite_ref-47" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Orca" title="Orca">Orcas</a> (<i>Orcinus orca</i>) are regular visitors around the islands.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Faroe_Islands_domestic_animals" title="Faroe Islands domestic animals">domestic animals of the Faroe Islands</a> are a result of 1,200 years of isolated breeding. As a result, many of the islands' domestic animals are found nowhere else in the world. Faroese domestic breeds include <a href="/wiki/Faroe_pony" title="Faroe pony">Faroe pony</a>, <a href="/wiki/Faroe_Islands_domestic_animals#Faroese_cow" title="Faroe Islands domestic animals">Faroe cow</a>, Faroe sheep, <a href="/wiki/Faroese_goose" title="Faroese goose">Faroese goose</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Faroe_Islands_domestic_animals#Faroese_duck" title="Faroe Islands domestic animals">Faroese duck</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Geology">Geology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Geology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Su%C3%B0uroy.FaroeIslands.2.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Su%C3%B0uroy.FaroeIslands.2.jpg/220px-Su%C3%B0uroy.FaroeIslands.2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Su%C3%B0uroy.FaroeIslands.2.jpg/330px-Su%C3%B0uroy.FaroeIslands.2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/62/Su%C3%B0uroy.FaroeIslands.2.jpg/440px-Su%C3%B0uroy.FaroeIslands.2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2560" data-file-height="1704" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Su%C3%B0uroy.FaroeIslands.2.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Beinisvørð, on the west coast of Suðuroy.</div></div></div>
<p>The islands were built up during a period characterised by high volcanic activity in the Early <a href="/wiki/Palaeogene" class="mw-redirect" title="Palaeogene">Palaeogene</a> around 50-60 million years ago. The islands are built up in layers of different lava flows (basalt) alternating with thin layers of volcanic ash (tuff). The soft ash and the hard basalt thus lie layer upon layer in narrow and thick strips. The soft tuff or ash zones erode away relatively quickly, and the hard lump of basalt above the eroded tuff falls away, forming the first terrace.
</p><p>Volcanic activity has varied over millions of years, with periods of quiescence and various periods of quiet eruptive fissures and explosive volcanism. In a few places, mainly on Suðuroy, thin layers of coal are present, which are the remains of swamp forests from the time between volcanic eruptions. The plateau has therefore been divided into different basalt series according to the course of volcanism and the age sequence of the layers.
</p><p>There are major differences in the shape of the islands' terraces. The lowest and oldest series are thick lava deposits that can be seen on the southern part of Suðuroy, Mykines, Tindhólmur and the western side of Vágar. The basalts of the lower basalt series are often pillared, which is shown by elongated, angular and regular pillars in the mountain side. Very regular vertical columns are found on northern Mykines, where they can be up to 30 metres (98&#160;ft) high.
</p><p>The middle basalt series consists of thin lava flows with a highly porous interlayer. This series has very little resistance to crumbling and weathering. As these erosion processes are more severe at higher altitudes than lower down, the lowlands are filled with weathering material from the heights, often resulting in a characteristic curved landscape shape. This can be clearly seen on Vágar, the northernmost part of Streymoy and on the north-western part of Eysturoy.
</p><p>Glacial activity has reduced plateau surfaces, especially on the northern islands, where the surfaces have been reduced to a series of narrower or wider zig-zag rows along the length of the islands. The phenomenon is particularly pronounced on the islands of Kunoy, Kalsoy and Borðoy, where an eastward and a westward ice mass have eroded the intervening mountain range into a narrow ridge.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Government and politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Politics of the Faroe Islands">Politics of the Faroe Islands</a></div>

srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Aksel_V_Johannesen_2015.JPG/225px-Aksel_V_Johannesen_2015.JPG 1.5x, class="thumbcaption"><a href="/wiki/Aksel_V._Johannesen" title="Aksel V. Johannesen">Aksel V. Johannesen</a>,<br /> <a href="/wiki/List_of_Lawmen_and_Prime_Ministers_of_the_Faroe_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Lawmen and Prime Ministers of the Faroe Islands">Faroese Prime Minister</a> since 2022</div></div></div></div></div><p>
The Faroe Islands are a self-governing nation under the external sovereignty of the Kingdom of Denmark.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> The Faroese government holds <a href="/wiki/Executive_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Executive power">executive power</a> in local government affairs. The head of the government is called the <i><a href="/wiki/L%C3%B8gma%C3%B0ur" class="mw-redirect" title="Løgmaður">Løgmaður</a></i> ("Chief Justice") and serves as Prime Minister and head of Faroese Government. Any other member of the cabinet is called a <i><a href="/wiki/Minister_(government)" title="Minister (government)">landsstýrismaður/ráðharri</a></i> ("Male Minister of the Faroese Government") or <i>landsstýriskvinna/ráðfrú</i> ("Female Minister of the Faroese Government"). The Faroese parliament – the <i><a href="/wiki/L%C3%B8gting" title="Løgting">Løgting</a></i> ("Law Thing") – dates back to the early days of settlement and claims to be one of the longest functioning parliaments in the world, alongside the Icelandic <a href="/wiki/Althing" title="Althing">Althing</a> and the Manx <a href="/wiki/Tynwald" title="Tynwald">Tynwald</a>. The parliament currently has 33 members.<sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup></p><div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Tinganes.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Tinganes.jpg/220px-Tinganes.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Tinganes.jpg/330px-Tinganes.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e7/Tinganes.jpg/440px-Tinganes.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="426" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Tinganes.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Tinganes" title="Tinganes">Tinganes</a> in <a href="/wiki/T%C3%B3rshavn" title="Tórshavn">Tórshavn</a>, seat of a part of the Faroese government</div></div></div>
<p>Elections are held at municipal and national levels, additionally electing two members to the <a href="/wiki/Folketing" title="Folketing">Folketing</a>. Until 2007, there were seven electoral districts, which were abolished on 25 October of that year in favour of a single nationwide district.
</p>
<div style="clear:both;"></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Regions_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Regions of the Faroe Islands">Regions of the Faroe Islands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Municipalities_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Municipalities of the Faroe Islands">Municipalities of the Faroe Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:292px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Map_of_the_Faroe_Islands_en.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Map_of_the_Faroe_Islands_en.svg/290px-Map_of_the_Faroe_Islands_en.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="380" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Map_of_the_Faroe_Islands_en.svg/435px-Map_of_the_Faroe_Islands_en.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b6/Map_of_the_Faroe_Islands_en.svg/580px-Map_of_the_Faroe_Islands_en.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1683" data-file-height="2208" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Map_of_the_Faroe_Islands_en.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Relief map of the Faroe Islands</div></div></div>
<p>Administratively, the islands are divided into 29 <a href="/wiki/Municipalities_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Municipalities of the Faroe Islands">municipalities</a> (<i>kommunur</i>) within which there are 120 or so <a href="/wiki/List_of_towns_in_the_Faroe_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="List of towns in the Faroe Islands">settlements</a>.
</p><p>There are also the six traditional <a href="/wiki/Regions_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Regions of the Faroe Islands">s<i>ýslur</i></a>: <a href="/wiki/Nor%C3%B0oyar" title="Norðoyar">Norðoyar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Eysturoy" title="Eysturoy">Eysturoy</a>, <a href="/wiki/Streymoy" title="Streymoy">Streymoy</a>, <a href="/wiki/V%C3%A1gar" title="Vágar">Vágar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sandoy" title="Sandoy">Sandoy</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Su%C3%B0uroy" title="Suðuroy">Suðuroy</a>. While no longer of any legal significance, the term is still commonly used to indicate a geographical region. In earlier times, each <i>sýsla</i> had its own <a href="/wiki/Thing_(assembly)" title="Thing (assembly)">assembly</a>, the so-called <i>várting</i> ("spring assembly").
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relationship_with_Denmark">Relationship with Denmark</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Relationship with Denmark">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a     </div>
</div><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The Faroe Islands have been under Norwegian-Danish control since 1388. The 1814 <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Kiel" title="Treaty of Kiel">Treaty of Kiel</a> terminated the <a href="/wiki/Denmark%E2%80%93Norway" title="Denmark–Norway">Danish–Norwegian</a> union, and Norway came under the rule of the <a href="/wiki/King_of_Sweden" class="mw-redirect" title="King of Sweden">King of Sweden</a>, while the Faroe Islands, Iceland, and <a href="/wiki/Greenland" title="Greenland">Greenland</a> remained Danish possessions. From ancient times the Faroe Islands had a parliament (<i>Løgting</i>), which was abolished in 1816, and the Faroe Islands were to be governed as an ordinary <a href="/wiki/Counties_of_Denmark" title="Counties of Denmark">Danish amt</a> (county), with the <a href="/wiki/Prefect" title="Prefect">Amtmand</a> as its head of government. In 1851, the <i>Løgting</i> was reinstated, but, until 1948, served mainly as an advisory body.
</p><p>The islands are home to a notable <a href="/wiki/Faroese_independence" class="mw-redirect" title="Faroese independence">independence movement</a> that has seen an increase in popular support within recent decades. At the end of <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, some of the population favoured independence from Denmark, and on 14 September 1946, <a href="/wiki/1946_Faroese_independence_referendum" title="1946 Faroese independence referendum">an independence referendum</a> was held on the question of <a href="/wiki/Secession" title="Secession">secession</a>. It was a consultative referendum, the parliament not being bound to follow the people's vote. This was the first time that the Faroese people had been asked whether they favoured independence or wanted to continue within the <a href="/wiki/Danish_kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Danish kingdom">Danish kingdom</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Queen_Margrethe_21-06-2005_V%C3%A1gur.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Queen_Margrethe_21-06-2005_V%C3%A1gur.jpg/220px-Queen_Margrethe_21-06-2005_V%C3%A1gur.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Queen_Margrethe_21-06-2005_V%C3%A1gur.jpg/330px-Queen_Margrethe_21-06-2005_V%C3%A1gur.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/Queen_Margrethe_21-06-2005_V%C3%A1gur.jpg/440px-Queen_Margrethe_21-06-2005_V%C3%A1gur.jpg 2x" data-file-width="640" data-file-height="426" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Queen_Margrethe_21-06-2005_V%C3%A1gur.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Queen <a href="/wiki/Margrethe_II_of_Denmark" title="Margrethe II of Denmark">Margrethe II</a>, monarch of the <a href="/wiki/Unity_of_the_Realm" class="mw-redirect" title="Unity of the Realm">Unity of the Realm</a>, during a visit to <a href="/wiki/V%C3%A1gur" title="Vágur">Vágur</a> in 2005</div></div></div>
<p>The result of the vote was only a slight majority in favour of secession. The Speaker of the Løgting, together with the majority, initiated the process of becoming an independent state. The minority of the Løgting left in protest, regarding these actions as illegal. One parliament member, Jákup í Jákupsstovu, was shunned by his own party, the Social Democratic Party, for having joined the majority of the Løgting.
</p><p>The Speaker of the Løgting declared the Faroe Islands independent on 18 September 1946.
</p><p>On 25 September 1946, a Danish prefect announced to the Løgting that the king, rejecting the majority vote, had dissolved the parliament and ordered new elections.
</p><p>A parliamentary election was held a few months later, in which the political parties that favoured remaining in the Danish kingdom increased their share of the vote and formed a coalition. Based on this, they chose to reject secession. Instead, a compromise was reached and the <a href="/wiki/Folketing" title="Folketing">Folketing</a> passed a home-rule law that went into effect in 1948. The Faroe Islands' status as a Danish <i>amt</i> was thereby brought to an end; the Faroe Islands were given a high degree of self-governance, supported by a financial <a href="/wiki/Subsidy" title="Subsidy">subsidy</a> from Denmark to recompense expenses the islands have on Danish services.
</p><p>In protest against the new Home Rule Act, <a href="/wiki/Republic_(Faroe_Islands)" title="Republic (Faroe Islands)">Republic (Tjóðveldi)</a>, was founded.
</p><p>At present, the islanders are about evenly split between those favouring independence and those who prefer to continue as a part of the Kingdom of Denmark.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Within both camps there is a wide range of opinions. Of those who favour independence, some are in favour of an immediate <a href="/wiki/Unilateral_declaration_of_independence" title="Unilateral declaration of independence">unilateral declaration of independence</a>. Others see independence as something to be attained gradually and with the full consent of the Danish government and the <a href="/wiki/Danish_nation" class="mw-redirect" title="Danish nation">Danish nation</a>. In the unionist camp, there are also many who foresee and welcome a gradual increase in autonomy even while strong ties with Denmark are maintained.
</p><p>Two attempts have been made to draft a separate Faroese constitution. The first time in 2011, when the then prime minister <a href="/wiki/Lars_L%C3%B8kke_Rasmussen" title="Lars Løkke Rasmussen">Lars Løkke Rasmussen</a> denounced it as incompatible with Denmark's constitution, stating that if the Faroe Islands wished to continue with the move, they must declare independence.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> A second attempt was made in 2015, facing similar criticisms<sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> before eventually being withdrawn without a vote.<sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relationship_with_the_European_Union">Relationship with the European Union</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Relationship with the European Union">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Faroe_Islands_and_the_European_Union" title="Faroe Islands and the European Union">Faroe Islands and the European Union</a></div>
<p>As explicitly asserted by both <a href="/wiki/Treaties_of_the_European_Union" title="Treaties of the European Union">treaties of the European Union</a>, the Faroe Islands are not part of the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>. The Faroes are not grouped with the EU when it comes to international trade; for instance, when the EU and Russia imposed reciprocal trade sanctions on each other over the <a href="/wiki/War_in_Donbas_(2014%E2%80%932022)" title="War in Donbas (2014–2022)">War in Donbas</a> in 2014, the Faroes began exporting significant amounts of fresh <a href="/wiki/Salmon" title="Salmon">salmon</a> to Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> Moreover, a <a href="/wiki/Special_member_state_territories_and_the_European_Union#Faroe_Islands" class="mw-redirect" title="Special member state territories and the European Union">protocol</a> to the treaty of accession of Denmark to the European Communities stipulates that Danish nationals residing in the Faroe Islands are not considered Danish nationals within the meaning of the treaties. Hence, Danish people living in the Faroes are not <a href="/wiki/Citizenship_of_the_European_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="Citizenship of the European Union">citizens of the European Union</a> (though other EU nationals living there remain EU citizens). The Faroes are not covered by the <a href="/wiki/Schengen_Agreement" title="Schengen Agreement">Schengen Agreement</a>, but there are no border checks when travelling between the Faroes and any Schengen country (the Faroes have been part of the <a href="/wiki/Nordic_Passport_Union" title="Nordic Passport Union">Nordic Passport Union</a> since 1966, and since 2001 there have been no permanent border checks between the Nordic countries and the rest of the <a href="/wiki/Schengen_Area" title="Schengen Area">Schengen Area</a> as part of the Schengen agreement).<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relationship_with_international_organisations">Relationship with international organisations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Relationship with international organisations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Faroe Islands are not fully independent, but they do have political relations directly with other countries through agreement with Denmark. The Faroe Islands are a member of some international organisations as though they were an independent country. The Faroes have associate membership in the <a href="/wiki/Nordic_Council" title="Nordic Council">Nordic Council</a> but have expressed wishes for full membership.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Faroe Islands are a member of several international sports federations like <a href="/wiki/UEFA" title="UEFA">UEFA</a>, <a href="/wiki/FIFA" title="FIFA">FIFA</a> in <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">football</a><sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> and <a href="/wiki/FINA" class="mw-redirect" title="FINA">FINA</a> in swimming<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> and EHF in <a href="/wiki/Handball" title="Handball">handball</a><sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> and have their own national teams. They also have their own telephone country code, <a href="/wiki/%2B298" class="mw-redirect" title="+298">+298</a>, Internet country code top-level domain, <a href="/wiki/.fo" title=".fo">.fo</a>, banking code FO and <a href="/wiki/Postal_codes_in_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Postal codes in the Faroe Islands">postal code system</a>.
</p><p>The Faroe Islands make their own agreements with other countries regarding trade and commerce. When the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">EU</a> embargo against <a href="/wiki/Russia" title="Russia">Russia</a> started in 2014, the Faroe Islands were not a part of the embargo because they are not a part of EU, and the islands had just themselves experienced a year of embargo from the EU including Denmark against the islands; the Faroese prime minister <a href="/wiki/Kaj_Leo_Johannesen" title="Kaj Leo Johannesen">Kaj Leo Johannesen</a> went to <a href="/wiki/Moscow" title="Moscow">Moscow</a> to negotiate the trade between Russia and the Faroe Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-Embargo2014_60-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Embargo2014-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/List_of_Ministers_of_Fisheries_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="List of Ministers of Fisheries of the Faroe Islands">Faroese minister of fisheries</a> negotiates with the EU and other countries regarding the rights to fish.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In mid-2005, representatives of the Faroe Islands raised the possibility of their territory joining the <a href="/wiki/European_Free_Trade_Association" title="European Free Trade Association">European Free Trade Association</a> (EFTA).<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> According to Article 56 of the EFTA Convention, only states may become members of the EFTA.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> The Faroes are an <a href="/wiki/Autonomous_administrative_division" title="Autonomous administrative division">autonomous territory</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Danish_Realm" title="Danish Realm">Kingdom of Denmark</a>, and not a <a href="/wiki/Sovereign_state" title="Sovereign state">sovereign state</a> in their own right.<sup id="cite_ref-faroes_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-faroes-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> Consequently, they considered the possibility that the "Kingdom of Denmark in respect of the Faroes" could join the EFTA, though the Danish Government has stated that this mechanism would not allow the Faroes to become a separate member of the EEA because Denmark was already a party to the EEA Agreement.<sup id="cite_ref-faroes_64-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-faroes-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Denmark" class="mw-redirect" title="Government of Denmark">Government of Denmark</a> officially supports new membership of the EFTA with effect for the Faroe Islands.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_the_Faroe_Islands" title="Demographics of the Faroe Islands">Demographics of the Faroe Islands</a></div>

style="text-align:right;padding:1px">+2.5%</td></tr><tr><th style="text-align:center;padding:1px">2020 </th><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">52,110</td><td style="text-align:right;padding:1px">+5.2%</td></tr><tr><td colspan="3" style="border-top:1px solid black;font-size:85%;text-align:left">2011 data<sup id="cite_ref-Statistics_Faroe_Islands_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Statistics_Faroe_Islands-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> 2019:<sup id="cite_ref-Faroe_Islands_in_figures_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Faroe_Islands_in_figures-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The vast majority of the population are <a href="/wiki/Faroe_Islanders" title="Faroe Islanders">ethnic Faroese</a>, of <a href="/wiki/Norsemen" title="Norsemen">Norse</a> and <a href="/wiki/Celts_(modern)" title="Celts (modern)">Celtic</a> descent.<sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> Recent DNA analyses have revealed that Y chromosomes, tracing male descent, are 87% <a href="/wiki/Scandinavia" title="Scandinavia">Scandinavian</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
The studies show that <a href="/wiki/Mitochondrial_DNA" title="Mitochondrial DNA">mitochondrial DNA</a>, tracing female descent, is 84% <a href="/wiki/Celts_(modern)" title="Celts (modern)">Celtic</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There is a gender deficit of about 2,000 women owing to migration.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> As a result, some Faroese men have married women from the <a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a> and <a href="/wiki/Thailand" title="Thailand">Thailand</a>, whom they met through such channels as online dating websites, and arranged for them to emigrate to the islands. This group of approximately three hundred women make up the largest ethnic minority in the Faroes.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_69-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> of the Faroe Islands is currently <a href="/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_and_dependent_territories_by_fertility_rate" class="mw-redirect" title="List of sovereign states and dependent territories by fertility rate">one of the highest</a> in Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> The fertility rate is 2.409 children born per woman (2015 est.).<sup id="cite_ref-Faroe_Islands_in_Figures_2016_71-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Faroe_Islands_in_Figures_2016-71">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The 2011 census shows that of the 48,346 inhabitants of the Faroe Islands (17,441 private households in 2011), 43,135 were born in the Faroe Islands, 3,597 were born elsewhere in the Kingdom of Denmark (Denmark proper or Greenland), and 1,614 were born outside the Kingdom of Denmark. People were also asked about their nationality, including Faroese. Children under 15 were not asked about their nationality. 97% said that they were ethnic Faroese, which means that many of those who were born in either Denmark or Greenland consider themselves as ethnic Faroese. The other 3% of those older than 15 said they were not Faroese: 515 were Danish, 433 were from other European countries, 147 came from Asia, 65 from Africa, 55 from the Americas, 23 from Russia.<sup id="cite_ref-2011_census,_born_in_other_countries_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2011_census,_born_in_other_countries-72">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> The Faroe Islands have people from 77 different nationalities.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Faroe_stamp_360_arrival_of_christianity.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f9/Faroe_stamp_360_arrival_of_christianity.jpg/170px-Faroe_stamp_360_arrival_of_christianity.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="251" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/f9/Faroe_stamp_360_arrival_of_christianity.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="244" data-file-height="360" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Faroe_stamp_360_arrival_of_christianity.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Faroese stamp by <a href="/wiki/Anker_Eli_Petersen" title="Anker Eli Petersen">Anker Eli Petersen</a> commemorating the arrival of Christianity in the islands</div></div></div>
<p>If the first inhabitants of the Faroe Islands were <a href="/wiki/Irish_people" title="Irish people">Irish</a> monks, they must have lived as a very small group of settlers. Later, when the Vikings colonised the islands, there was a considerable increase in the population. However, it never exceeded 5,000 until the 19th century. Around 1349, about half the population perished in the <a href="/wiki/Black_Death" title="Black Death">Black Death</a> plague.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Only with the rise of the deep-sea fishery (and thus independence from agriculture in the islands' harsh terrain) and with general progress in the health service was rapid population growth possible in the Faroes. Beginning in the 19th century, the population increased tenfold in 200 years.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>At the beginning of the 1990s, the Faroe Islands entered a deep economic crisis leading to heavy emigration; however, this trend reversed in subsequent years to a net immigration. This has been in the form of a population replacement as young Faroese women leave and are replaced with Asian/Pacific brides.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup>
In 2011, there were 2,155 more men than women between the age of 0 to 59 in the Faroe Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-74" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-74">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Faroese population is spread across most of the area; it was not until recent decades that significant urbanisation occurred. Industrialisation has been remarkably decentralised. Nevertheless, villages with poor harbour facilities have been the losers in the development from agriculture to fishing, and in the most peripheral agricultural areas, also known as <a href="/wiki/%C3%9Atoyggjar" title="Útoyggjar">Útoyggjar</a> ("Outer Islands"), there are few young people. In recent decades, the village-based social structure has given way to a rise in interconnected "centres" that are better able to provide goods and services than the badly connected periphery. Shops and services are relocating en masse from the villages into the centres, and slowly but steadily the Faroese population is concentrating in and around the centres.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>In the 1990s, the government abandoned the old national policy of developing the villages (<i>Bygdamenning</i>), and instead began a process of regional development (<i>Økismenning</i>). The term "region" referred to the large islands of the Faroes. Nevertheless, the government was unable to press through the structural reform of merging small rural municipalities to create sustainable, decentralised entities that could drive forward regional development. As regional development has been difficult on the administrative level, the government has instead invested heavily in infrastructure, interconnecting the regions.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
