</p><p>This tradition binds together families, passes on traditional crafts, and reinforces the Faroese culture of traditional village life in the context of a modern society.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Archives">Archives</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Archives">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The National Archives of the Faroe Islands (Faroese: Tjóðskjalasavnið) is located in Tórshavn. Their main task is to collect, organize, record and preserve the archival records (documents) of the authorities, in order to make them available to the public in the future. In this context, the National Archives supervises the register (diary) and archives of the public authorities. Currently, there are no other permanent archives in the Faroe Islands, but since the end of 2017, the national government has provided financial support for a three-year pilot project under the name "Tvøroyrar Skjalasavn", which aims to collect private archives from the area.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Libraries">Libraries</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Libraries">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The National Library of the Faroe Islands (in Faroese: Føroya Landsbókasavn) is based in Tórshavn and its main task is to collect, record, preserve and disseminate knowledge of literature related to the Faroe Islands. The <a href="/wiki/National_library" title="National library">National Library</a> also functions as a research library and public library. In addition to the National Library, there are 15 municipal libraries and 11 school libraries in the Faroe Islands.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Museums_and_galleries">Museums and galleries</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Museums and galleries">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The Faroe Islands has numerous museums and galleries.
</p><p>Føroya Fornminnissavn, Historical Museum; Listasavn Førøya, Faroese Museum of Art; Náttúrugripasavnið, Faroese Museum of Natural History; Norðurlandahúsið, House of the North; Heima á Garði, Hoyvík, Open Air Museum in Hoyvík; Føroya Sjósavn, Faroese Aquarium in Argir; Galerie Focus, Glarsmiðjan; Listagluggin, Art Gallery.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Visual_arts">Visual arts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Visual arts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Faroese visual art is of great importance for the memory of Faroese <a href="/wiki/National_identity" title="National identity">national identity</a>, as well as for the dissemination of the Faroese visual universe.
</p><p>The different periods and expressions of the visual arts meet and complement each other, but can also create a tension between the past and the present form of expression.
</p><p>Faroese stamps designed by Faroese artists are currently on offer.
</p><p>The first Faroese art exhibition was held in <a href="/wiki/T%C3%B3rshavn" title="Tórshavn">Tórshavn</a> in 1927.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Artists_visiting_the_Faroe_Islands">Artists visiting the Faroe Islands</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Artists visiting the Faroe Islands">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Ingo_K%C3%BChl_%22F%C3%A4r%C3%B6er_II%22,_%C3%96l_auf_Nessel_120_x_120_cm,_1995,_Werkverzeichnis_38-95.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/Ingo_K%C3%BChl_%22F%C3%A4r%C3%B6er_II%22%2C_%C3%96l_auf_Nessel_120_x_120_cm%2C_1995%2C_Werkverzeichnis_38-95.jpg/220px-Ingo_K%C3%BChl_%22F%C3%A4r%C3%B6er_II%22%2C_%C3%96l_auf_Nessel_120_x_120_cm%2C_1995%2C_Werkverzeichnis_38-95.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="223" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/Ingo_K%C3%BChl_%22F%C3%A4r%C3%B6er_II%22%2C_%C3%96l_auf_Nessel_120_x_120_cm%2C_1995%2C_Werkverzeichnis_38-95.jpg/330px-Ingo_K%C3%BChl_%22F%C3%A4r%C3%B6er_II%22%2C_%C3%96l_auf_Nessel_120_x_120_cm%2C_1995%2C_Werkverzeichnis_38-95.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/Ingo_K%C3%BChl_%22F%C3%A4r%C3%B6er_II%22%2C_%C3%96l_auf_Nessel_120_x_120_cm%2C_1995%2C_Werkverzeichnis_38-95.jpg/440px-Ingo_K%C3%BChl_%22F%C3%A4r%C3%B6er_II%22%2C_%C3%96l_auf_Nessel_120_x_120_cm%2C_1995%2C_Werkverzeichnis_38-95.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3442" data-file-height="3487" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Ingo_K%C3%BChl_%22F%C3%A4r%C3%B6er_II%22,_%C3%96l_auf_Nessel_120_x_120_cm,_1995,_Werkverzeichnis_38-95.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Ingo_K%C3%BChl" title="Ingo Kühl">Ingo Kühl</a> <i>Faroe Islands II</i>, Oilpainting, 120 x 120 cm, 1995</div></div></div>
<p>In 1890 the German painter Alf Bachmann visited the Faroe Islands.<sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1995 the German artist <a href="/wiki/Ingo_K%C3%BChl" title="Ingo Kühl">Ingo Kühl</a> painted watercolours in Gjógv, after which the nine-part Faroe Islands picture cycle was created, which was exhibited in the Royal Danish Embassy in Berlin in 2003/2004.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cinema">Cinema</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Cinema">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Faroese filmmakers have made several short films in particular in recent decades, and Katrin Ottarsdóttir, among others, has directed three feature films, several shorts and documentaries since her debut in 1989 with Rhapsody of the Atlantic. In 2012, the Faroese Geytin Film Award was established. These are two film awards that are presented once a year at a film festival at the Nordic House in Tórshavn in <a href="/wiki/December" title="December">December</a>. Filmmakers enter their films and a committee selects up to 10 films, which are screened at the event at the Nordic House. The main prize, worth DKK 25,000 and a statuette, is called Geytin and is awarded by the Nordic House, while the second prize, the <a href="/wiki/Audience_award" title="Audience award">Audience Award</a> (Áskoðaravirðislønin), is worth DKK 15,000 and is awarded by the Thorshavn City Council. Sakaris Stórá won the first Geytin in December 2012 with the film Summarnátt (Summer Night).
</p><p>In February 2014, his film Vetrarmorgun (Winter Morning) won three awards at the <a href="/wiki/Berlin_International_Film_Festival" title="Berlin International Film Festival">Berlinale</a>. In 2012, Annika á Lofti won the Audience Award.In 2013, Olaf Johannessen won a Robert for Best Supporting Actor in the TV series Forbrydelsen III. In 2013 Dávur Djurhuus Geytin won for the short film Terminal, while Jónfinn Stenberg won the Audience Award for the short film Munch. In 2014, the same person won both film awards, as Heiðrikur á Heygum won both the Geytin and the Audience Award for the 30-minute horror film Skuld (Guilt) Andrias Høgenni won both awards at the Geytin in 2016 for the short film A Crack.
</p><p>In 2019, he won the top prize at Geytin for the <a href="/wiki/Short_film" title="Short film">short film</a> Ikki illa meint. The same film, which was his graduation film in Super 16, was awarded at the Cannes Film Festival, Semaine de la Critique, and also won Danish film awards such as the Robert for Best Short Film and the fiction award at the Ekko Shortlist Awards.
</p><p>In 2014, the Faroese <a href="/wiki/Culture_minister" title="Culture minister">Ministry of Culture</a> received a grant in the Finance Act to provide financial support for Faroese films. In 2017, Filmshúsið was established. Filmshúsið is located in Sjóvinnuhúsið in Tórshavn. They will guide and assist the Faroese film community and market Faroese films abroad and assist film productions. The Klippfisk film workshop is also located in Sjóvinnuhúsið. Klippfisk is supported by the municipality of Tórshavn and works with young film talent, including organizing the annual Nóllywood film school for teenagers. Nóllywood is held on the island of Nólsoy, usually during the summer vacations.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Public_holidays">Public holidays</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Faroe_Islands&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: Public holidays">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:F%C3%B8roysk_fl%C3%B8gg_%C3%A1_%C3%93lavs%C3%B8ku.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/F%C3%B8roysk_fl%C3%B8gg_%C3%A1_%C3%93lavs%C3%B8ku.jpg/220px-F%C3%B8roysk_fl%C3%B8gg_%C3%A1_%C3%93lavs%C3%B8ku.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="232" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/F%C3%B8roysk_fl%C3%B8gg_%C3%A1_%C3%93lavs%C3%B8ku.jpg/330px-F%C3%B8roysk_fl%C3%B8gg_%C3%A1_%C3%93lavs%C3%B8ku.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/f/fd/F%C3%B8roysk_fl%C3%B8gg_%C3%A1_%C3%93lavs%C3%B8ku.jpg 2x" data-file-width="429" data-file-height="452" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:F%C3%B8roysk_fl%C3%B8gg_%C3%A1_%C3%93lavs%C3%B8ku.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The annual <i>Ólavsøka</i> parade on 28 July 2005.</div></div></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Denmark" title="Public holidays in Denmark">Public holidays in Denmark</a></div>
<p><i><a href="/wiki/%C3%93lavs%C3%B8ka" title="Ólavsøka">Ólavsøka</a></i> is on 29 July; it commemorates the death of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Olaf" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Olaf">Saint Olaf</a>. The celebrations are held in Tórshavn, starting on the evening of the 28th and continuing until the 31st. 28 July is a half working day for the members of some of the labour unions, while Ólavsøkudagur (St Olaf's Day) on 29 July is a full holiday for most but not all union members.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The official celebration starts on the 29th, with the opening of the <a href="/wiki/Faroese_Parliament" class="mw-redirect" title="Faroese Parliament">Faroese Parliament</a>, a custom that dates back 900 years.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> This begins with a service held in <a href="/wiki/T%C3%B3rshavn_Cathedral" title="Tórshavn Cathedral">Tórshavn Cathedral</a>; all members of parliament as well as civil and church officials walk to the cathedral in a procession. All of the parish ministers take turns giving the sermon. After the service, the procession returns to the parliament for the opening ceremony.
</p><p>Other celebrations are marked by different kinds of sports competitions, the rowing competition (in Tórshavn Harbour) being the most popular, art exhibitions, pop concerts, and the famous <a href="/wiki/Faroese_dance" class="mw-redirect" title="Faroese dance">Faroese dance</a> in Sjónleikarhúsið and on Vaglið outdoor singing on 29 July (continuing after midnight on 30 July). The celebrations have many facets, and only a few are mentioned here.
</p><p>Many people also mark the occasion by wearing the national Faroese dress.
</p>
